The recovery scenario uses a newly registered account, an unknown email, a compliant replacement such as “Replacement-password-456!”, and invalid code “000000”; failure leaves the registered account able to sign in with its old email/password. In this flow, the local system does not send email, generate a copyable reset link, or call an external verification-code service. To prevent disclosure through the page of whether an account exists, both registered and unknown email addresses enter the same next step and display the same fixed verification code. The system atomically updates the credentials of that account only when the recovery context is associated with a registered email, the Verification code is character-for-character equal to “123456”, the new password complies with the password rules in REQ-1-1-1, and Confirm password matches it. This local verification code has no email-delivery, expiration, or one-time-link semantics. The page contains exactly one button named “Account menu”; its menu contains exactly one link named “Sign out”. Activating it displays a dialog named “Sign out” with buttons named “Confirm sign out” and “Cancel”. The dialog explains that sign-out affects only the current browser session. Only “Confirm sign out” invalidates the session, while “Cancel” or closing the dialog retains the current session and page. After confirming, refresh, browser back navigation, or directly reopening a previously accessible protected account, repository, or organization page restores an unauthenticated state and displays the “Sign in” link.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-account-menu-sign-out.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-1-2: Sign Out and End the Current Web Session - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The security form has uniquely labeled password inputs “Current password”, “New password”, and “Confirm password”, and a button “Update password”; submitting an empty current-password field must display the visible application message “Current password is required”. A failure with an incorrect current password and confirmation “does-not-match” must display the corresponding current-password or confirmation error and leave the old credentials usable. Each password-change scenario starts with its own verified account in the stated credential state and a different compliant candidate password. “Password and authentication” is the security page in account Settings used to change the current account credentials; the change affects only the current account's password record and does not alter any other accounts or resources outside the session. The user must provide the current password, a new password compliant with the REQ-1 password rules, and a character-for-character identical confirmation password; all three fields are required. The system validates the change, prevents unauthorized updates, and applies the new password to subsequent sign-in attempts. If the current password is incorrect, the new password is noncompliant, the confirmation does not match, or any field is missing, the system displays the reason in the corresponding field and does not modify the credentials; the applicable visible message is “Current password is incorrect”, “Password confirmation does not match”, or “Current password is required”. The old password remains valid and the new password must not work for sign-in. After a successful update, the page displays “Password updated”, the new password is immediately used for subsequent password-based sign-ins, and the old password must no longer work for new sign-ins.\n\nSeed password-change values are `New-password-456!` for the successful update and\n`Required-password-789!` for the missing-current-password scenario.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-password-settings.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-1-3: Change Account Password - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Filtering updates the visible results as the user types, without a separate submit action. Each result has a link named exactly with the repository name, and opens a heading displaying “organization name/repository name”. Returning with browser Back keeps the public result available; filtering by the exact private repository name never exposes its link to a visitor. The seed organization has a public repository and a distinct private repository inaccessible to visitors. From the organization page, the user clicks the “Repositories” link, can enter a repository name in the filter box, select a public/private filter, and click a repository name in the list to open its overview. The system displays only the names, descriptions, visibility, and update times of repositories visible to the current user; clicking a result opens that repository's overview. Visitors can view public repositories on a public organization overview page; signed-in accounts can see only repositories for which they have read permission on the Repositories tab of the organization overview. The list reads repository name, description, visibility, and update time and provides no write operations; private repositories must first be validated under the already defined repository-access rules.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-organization-repositories.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-2-1-1: Browse Organization Repositories - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': \"The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Its form has fields labeled “Organization name” and “Display name” and a button “Create organization”. The new overview uses a heading containing the organization identifier, which remains after reload. Submitting an existing identifier must show “Organization name already exists” even when the display name is also missing; it must not navigate to a heading for the existing organization. The malformed identifier “-invalid-organization” and a whitespace-only display name are rejected with the applicable field error. Seed data supplies a verified creator and an existing organization identifier for the duplicate case. A signed-in user enters a unique organization name and display information on the organization-creation page and confirms. The organization name must comply with the 1–39-character lowercase ASCII REQ-1 username format and be globally unique; the display name must contain 1–100 non-empty characters after trimming leading and trailing whitespace. The system validates these rules, creates the organization identifier, display name, and creation time, and saves the current user as both initial owner and member; if the name or display name is noncompliant, the name conflicts, or persistence fails, the system retains non-sensitive input, displays the reason in the corresponding field, and does not create the organization. The applicable visible message is “Organization name already exists”, “Organization name format is invalid”, or “Display name is required”. On success, the user is redirected to the new organization overview page, the organization appears in the current user's accessible-organization list, and subsequent team, member, and repository authorization is scoped by that organization identifier. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-2-1-2: Create an Organization After Authentication - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. A unique compliant name can be submitted without a description or parent. The resulting team heading contains its name and remains visible after reload. Seed data supplies an organization and an account with Owner permission. A team page is the detail page for a grouping of accounts within an organization, titled “organization name/team name”; the Members tab displays the team's direct members, and Settings is used to change the parent team. Only an organization Owner can click “New team” from the Teams tab on the organization overview. The system stores the team identifier, owning organization, name, optional description, optional parent team, creator, and timestamp; if the team name is missing, malformed, duplicated, the parent team does not belong to the current organization, or creation fails, no team is generated. A malformed team name displays “Team name format is invalid”. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-2-1-2 (Create an Organization After Authentication); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-2-2-1: Create an Organization Team - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The team maintainer in these scenarios is an organization Owner. The team page has links “Members” and “Settings”. On Members, the button “Add member” opens a textbox labeled “Username” and the submit button “Add member”; only the current step's Add member button is exposed as an actionable match. After adding, the member's complete username is visible once in the member list, alongside a button named “Remove <username>”. Activating this removal button immediately removes that member without a second confirmation step, and reload keeps the username absent. Settings contains a native select with combobox role labeled “Parent team”, whose option labels are team names, and a button “Save”. The original parent remains selected after a rejected change and reload. The cycle scenario supplies a team with an existing parent and a descendant selectable by name; submitting that descendant shows the cycle error rather than saving it. The seed record's original-parent value identifies the stored selection, which may differ from its display label. The membership scenario supplies a current organization member not yet in this team. On the team page, the Owner clicks “Members” to add or remove current organization members, or selects a new parent team in “Settings” and saves. The system stores the team-membership relationship or team hierarchy and immediately reflects it in the organization team tree and member list; team hierarchy creates no implicit membership relationship or repository permission, and cyclic hierarchy is prohibited. A rejected cycle displays “Cyclic team hierarchy is not allowed”. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-2-2-1 (Create an Organization Team); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-2-2-2: Manage Organization Team Members and Hierarchy - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n\n[Showing lines 1-193 of 877 (50.0KB limit). This product directly marks the email of a successfully registered account as verified and does not provide a subsequent email-verification page. The visitor enters a unique username, email, password, and matching confirmation, checks “Agree to the terms”, and activates “Create account”. The username must consist of 1 to 39 lowercase ASCII letters, digits, or single hyphens (`-`) and must not begin or end with a hyphen; after trimming leading and trailing whitespace, the email must contain exactly one `@`, be no longer than 254 characters, and have at least one dot and non-empty domain labels after the `@`; the password must contain 12 to 128 characters, include at least one uppercase letter, one lowercase letter, one digit, and one non-alphanumeric special character, and contain no whitespace characters. To prevent disclosure through the page of whether an account exists, both registered and unknown email addresses enter the same next step and display the same fixed verification code. The system atomically updates the credentials of that account only when the recovery context is associated with a registered email, the Verification code is character-for-character equal to “123456”, the new password complies with the password rules in REQ-1-1-1, and Confirm password matches it. After successful submission, the page directly displays “Password updated” and does not produce any email or link. This local verification code has no email-delivery, expiration, or one-time-link semantics. Password fields must not echo entered values.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-password-reset.png) ![image](reference/github-password-reset-1.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-1-1-3: Recover Account Access Through a Verified Email - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The security form has uniquely labeled password inputs “Current password”, “New password”, and “Confirm password”, and a button “Update password”; submitting an empty current-password field must display the visible application message “Current password is required”. A failure with an incorrect current password and confirmation “does-not-match” must display the corresponding current-password or confirmation error and leave the old credentials usable. Each password-change scenario starts with its own verified account in the stated credential state and a different compliant candidate password. “Password and authentication” is the security page in account Settings used to change the current account credentials; the change affects only the current account's password record and does not alter any other accounts or resources outside the session. The user must provide the current password, a new password compliant with the REQ-1 password rules, and a character-for-character identical confirmation password; all three fields are required. The system validates the change, prevents unauthorized updates, and applies the new password to subsequent sign-in attempts. If the current password is incorrect, the new password is noncompliant, the confirmation does not match, or any field is missing, the system displays the reason in the corresponding field and does not modify the credentials; the applicable visible message is “Current password is incorrect”, “Password confirmation does not match”, or “Current password is required”. The old password remains valid and the new password must not work for sign-in. After a successful update, the page displays “Password updated”, the new password is immediately used for subsequent password-based sign-ins, and the old password must no longer work for new sign-ins.\n\nSeed password-change values are `New-password-456!` for the successful update and\n`Required-password-789!` for the missing-current-password scenario.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-password-settings.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-1-3: Change Account Password - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: organization `Acme Demo`, repository `acme-docs`, member `bob-reviewer`, team `frontend-team`.'}]\n\n## REQ-2-2-4: Remove a Member from an Organization\n\nOpen the organization's “People” link to read member usernames. Each removable member has a button named “Member menu <username>”; it opens the menuitem “Remove from organization”, followed by the confirmation button “Remove”. Successful removal makes the complete username absent from People immediately and after reload. A non-Owner viewing the same member has no member-menu button and no Remove from organization menuitem, rather than merely a disabled control. Each independent scenario starts with that member present; the non-Owner is an ordinary organization member. On the “People” page, an organization Owner locates a member, clicks “Remove from organization” in that member's action menu, and clicks “Remove” in the confirmation dialog. The system atomically deletes that member's organization-membership relationship, all of the account's memberships in teams of that organization, and all direct grants to that account on repositories of the organization; if the account still obtains repository permission through another organization Owner status or an undeleted team grant, effective permission is recalculated according to the valid permission rules, otherwise access is denied. The system must not delete the personal account, personal repositories, relationships with other organizations, teams themselves, or team grants; the last Owner must not be removed. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-2-1-2 (Create an Organization After Authentication); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-2-2-4: Remove a Member from an Organization - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': \"The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The picker presents a textbox “Search”, a matching selectable team option whose name contains the team name, a combobox “Role” with clickable role options including “Write”, and an “Add” button. While the picker is active, hide the opening Add people or teams button from the active view so the submit action is unambiguous. After saving, the authorization list visibly shows the exact team name and Write role and retains the team after reload. Existing grants appear as rows whose accessible names include the subject name; each row contains a native select labeled “Role” and a “Save” button. Selecting the option labeled “Read” and saving replaces Write; after reload exactly one row for the team remains and contains Read. The creation scenario supplies a repository Admin and an organization team not yet granted access; the replacement scenario supplies a separate repository/team with an existing Write grant. An organization Owner or repository Admin may grant a repository role of Read, Triage, Write, Maintain, or Admin to a current organization member or team. In the access-subject picker, matching member or team options update as the administrator types; pressing Enter or activating a separate search button is not required before selecting an option. A private repository is accessible only to an organization Owner, a member with a direct repository role, or a direct member of a team that has a repository role; an ordinary organization Member has no default private-repository access. For a given subject and repository, only one direct role grant is stored — saving the same role again does not create a second record, and changing the role replaces the original role. An account's effective repository role is the highest among Admin from Owner status and all still-valid direct grants and team grants; team hierarchy does not propagate membership or authorization. The system stores the authorization subject, repository, role, grantor, and timestamp and authorizes operations according to the ROOT role matrix; unauthorized accounts cannot see private repositories. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-2-1-2 (Create an Organization After Authentication) and REQ-2-2-1 (Create an Organization Team); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-2-3: Grant Repository Access to People and Teams - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Visitors or signed-in users can search repositories they are authorized to view. The signed-in workspace provides a “New repository” link. The form labels are “Owner”, “Repository name”, and “Description”; the signed-in user's personal namespace is selected by default, so it is possible to submit without changing the owner. Visibility choices are radio controls “Public” and “Private”; initialization is a checkbox “Add a README file”; submission uses the button “Create repository”. Other defaults must allow duplicate-name and empty-name validation without further input. A successful initialized private repository shows a heading containing the new name, a visible Private marker, and a README file link; the overview persists after reload. The optional description “Repository created by Playwright” is saved when entered. An existing repository in this same default personal namespace is supplied for duplicate validation, which stays on the form and does not open the existing repository heading. On the repository-creation page, a signed-in user selects a personal or organization owner, enters a repository name and description, selects public or private visibility, and chooses whether to create an initial commit with a README. The system verifies that the user may create a repository in that owner's namespace, that the name is unique, and that the options are valid; on success, it stores the repository identifier, owner, visibility, default branch, and creator. If initialization is selected, it must also atomically create the initial branch, README file, and initial commit; failure at any step must leave no partially created repository. On success, the new repository overview page is opened.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-create-repository.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-3-2-1: Create a Repository with Owner, Visibility, and Initialization Options - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The fork form contains a field labeled “Repository name” and button “Create fork”; it defaults to the signed-in user's personal namespace and an allowed visibility, permitting submission after editing only the name. Seed data supplies a readable source, a user allowed to create personal repositories, and an existing fork name in that namespace for the conflict case. Success opens a heading containing the new fork name and visible text “Forked from <source repository name>” with a source link; the heading and source relationship survive reload. On the source repository page, the user clicks “Fork”, selects a target personal or organization namespace, enters an optional new name and visibility, and clicks “Create fork”. The system creates an independent fork, copies the accessible default-branch history, records the source-repository link, and then opens the new fork overview page. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. A signed-in user can fork only when they have Read or higher permission on the source repository and permission to create repositories in the target personal or organization namespace; when the source repository is private, the fork must remain Private, while a public source repository may be forked as Public or Private. The system validates permissions on both ends and stores the new fork's owner, visibility, source-repository identifier, default branch, and copied accessible history; if any check fails, no fork is created.\n\nThe fork conflict seed uses the existing name `acme-docs-fork` in the target\npersonal namespace.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-fork-repository.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-3-2-2: Fork a Repository into Another Namespace - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Protocol controls are tabs “HTTPS” and “SSH”, and the selected clone value has a button “Copy clone value”. The HTTPS clone value uses the HTTPS protocol; the SSH clone value uses the selected SSH format, including the colon and .git suffix. Both must identify the current repository. With browser clipboard permission, copying writes the selected complete value, displays “Copied”, and leaves the repository heading visible. Seed data provides a visitor-readable public repository; clipboard permission is a browser precondition. On the repository overview page, the user clicks “Code”, selects the HTTPS or SSH clone method, and then clicks the copy icon to the right of the clone value. The system writes the selected clone value to the browser clipboard and displays brief “Copied” feedback; this read-only operation does not modify the repository. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Visitors or signed-in users may use this capability on a repository page. This capability requires the target public object to be available or the user to have view permission. The system verifies eligibility, displays any required warning or unavailable state, and prevents unauthorized changes. The selected repository-access value can be pasted by the user into a local tool.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-code-clone-menu.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-3-2-3: Copy a Repository Clone Value - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The picker presents a textbox “Search”, a matching selectable team option whose name contains the team name, a combobox “Role” with clickable role options including “Write”, and an “Add” button. While the picker is active, hide the opening Add people or teams button from the active view so the submit action is unambiguous. After saving, the authorization list visibly shows the exact team name and Write role and retains the team after reload. Existing grants appear as rows whose accessible names include the subject name; each row contains a native select labeled “Role” and a “Save” button. Selecting the option labeled “Read” and saving replaces Write; after reload exactly one row for the team remains and contains Read. The creation scenario supplies a repository Admin and an organization team not yet granted access; the replacement scenario supplies a separate repository/team with an existing Write grant. An organization Owner or repository Admin may grant a repository role of Read, Triage, Write, Maintain, or Admin to a current organization member or team. In the access-subject picker, matching member or team options update as the administrator types; pressing Enter or activating a separate search button is not required before selecting an option. A private repository is accessible only to an organization Owner, a member with a direct repository role, or a direct member of a team that has a repository role; an ordinary organization Member has no default private-repository access. For a given subject and repository, only one direct role grant is stored — saving the same role again does not create a second record, and changing the role replaces the original role. An account's effective repository role is the highest among Admin from Owner status and all still-valid direct grants and team grants; team hierarchy does not propagate membership or authorization. The system stores the authorization subject, repository, role, grantor, and timestamp and authorizes operations according to the ROOT role matrix; unauthorized accounts cannot see private repositories. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-2-1-2 (Create an Organization After Authentication) and REQ-2-2-1 (Create an Organization Team); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-2-3: Grant Repository Access to People and Teams - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.'}]\n\n## REQ-4-2-2: Inspect Commit and Revision Differences\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-2-1 (View Repository Commit History); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Any user with repository-view permission can compare two readable commits or revisions on the diff page; the page reads and displays the changed files and line-by-line additions and deletions between them without creating a review, comment, or commit. A seeded commit has an accessible commit entry, a known changed-file path `src/search.ts`, and a readable parent revision. Opening that commit entry as a visitor displays the changed-file path as an exact text value, the “Changed files” summary, and the numeric additions/deletions summary described below, without requiring previous navigation through history.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-commit-diff.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-4-2-2: Inspect Commit and Revision Differences - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The system displays matching snippets, file paths, and branch/revision context within the visible code scope of the current repository; clicking a result opens the corresponding file location. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-1 (Browse Repository Files and Directories); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Visitors can search from the Code page of a public repository, while signed-in accounts can search any repository they are authorized to view; the results page only reads file content, matching snippets, and paths from currently visible branches of the current repository and cannot leak results across repositories or write code. The repository page and search results expose one searchbox named “Search”; after Enter, a unique link named “Code” selects code results. This results-type link must remain distinguishable from repository navigation so the active search page has only one Code link. Path and language filters are optional, and an unfiltered search can immediately open a result link whose exact accessible name is the matching file name. Seed data provides the known query `search flow`, a matching file `README.md` with that query visible as a complete text value, and the absent query `no-such-token`, which does not occur in searchable code. Opening the matching file shows that text; refreshing preserves the file context, text, and a link named exactly after the file. For the absent query, show “No code results” and retain the exact query in Search. Returning to the repository and repeating the same search produces the same empty state without stale matches.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-repository-code-search.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-4-2-3: Search Code Within a Repository - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Matching branch options or the “No matching branch” state update as the user types in the branch textbox; pressing Enter or activating a separate search button is not required. The selector is a unique button named “Branch <current branch name>”; it opens a textbox named “Find branch” and selectable items with role option whose exact accessible names are the branch names. Seed data includes active branch `main`, target branch `feature-search` containing the file `main-only.md` absent from `main`, and an unknown search term matching no branch. Selecting the target immediately updates the button's branch name and shows a link with the exact target-only file name. Escape closes the selector; for an unmatched search it preserves the original active branch before and after reload.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-branch-selector.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-4-3-1: List and Switch Repository Branches - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. As the user types, a valid unused name displays the “Create branch: <name>” option without requiring Enter or a separate search action, while an invalid name immediately displays “Invalid branch”. The system creates a new branch pointing to that base commit and switches the selector to it; if the name already exists or the user lacks permission, no branch is created. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only signed-in users with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status may create branches on repository code and version-control pages; Read and Triage may only browse. The system validates the role, stores the new branch name, base commit, creator, and time, and rejects unauthorized requests. The selector and “Find branch” textbox use REQ-4-3-1's roles. For a valid unused name, the creation entry has role option and accessible name “Create branch: <name>”. Its displayed base defaults to the current branch head; selecting this option performs creation and switches branches without a second mandatory confirmation. Reloading the resulting page entry keeps the newly created branch selected. In particular, entering `invalid..branch` immediately shows “Invalid branch” and cannot create a reference; generated valid names such as `pw-branch-<unique suffix>` follow the same rules.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-create-branch.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-4-3-2: Create a Branch from an Existing Revision - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': \"The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The system saves the new default branch and updates the initial branch shown when the repository is newly opened; it does not delete or rewrite the previous default branch. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only a repository Admin or organization Owner may change the default branch on repository code and version-control pages; other roles may only view. The system validates the role, stores the new default branch together with the operator and time, and rejects unauthorized requests. “Settings” and “Branches” are unique links on their respective pages; “Default branch” is a native HTML select element exposing the combobox role, with options labeled by exact existing branch names; selecting an option must use the native selection behavior. The administrator selects a different seeded branch, activates the “Update” button, and then the “Confirm” button in the confirmation dialog. Opening the repository page entry without a branch then shows “Branch <new default branch name>”; opening that selector still offers an option named exactly after the old default branch. A non-Admin may have Settings or Branches links hidden or may open a read-only settings page, but no “Default branch” combobox or default-branch update button is rendered for that user; a merely disabled editable selector is insufficient.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-default-branch-settings.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-4-3-3: Change the Repository Default Branch - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The file editor is the form opened from Edit or Add file on the Code page; the user edits file content at a path on the current branch and submits it. Only Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner may submit; Read and Triage may only view. A single submission stores the file-path and content change, commit message, author, parent commit, and target branch as one indivisible record and moves the target branch to point to that new commit. A new or renamed file path must not be empty, must not begin with `/`, must not contain a `..` path segment, and must not conflict with an existing file or directory on the current branch; the commit message must contain 1–72 non-empty characters after trimming leading and trailing whitespace. If the path conflicts, content validation fails, the commit message is noncompliant, the branch is protected, or persistence fails, the page displays the reason and does not change the file, branch head, or commit history. An invalid path displays “Invalid file path”, and an empty commit message displays “Commit message is required”. From the writable Code page, the unique “Add file” button opens the “Create new file” menuitem. The editor has a field labeled “File name”, a textbox named “File contents”, an initially empty field labeled “Commit message”, and a “Commit changes” button. Creating a unique file such as `pw-file-<unique suffix>.md` with nonempty text and a message such as `Add <file name>` immediately opens a view displaying the exact saved content. Its “Commits” link opens history displaying the exact submitted message. Submitting `../invalid.md` with content `must not be saved` and no commit message reports the invalid path and/or required message and changes neither files nor history. The example content is ordinary text, not a forbidden content value by itself; rejection follows from the invalid path or missing message.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-web-file-editor.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-4-4: Manage Repository Files Through the Web Interface - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.'}]\n\n## REQ-5-1-1: List and Filter Repository Issues\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Users with repository-view permission can read issue rows for the current repository on the Issues list page; each row references a persisted issue number and displays the title, status, author, labels, and update time. Users can filter by Open/Closed, title or body keywords, and labels; when the user types in the issue search box, the matching issue rows update without requiring Enter or a separate search button. Filtering does not create or modify work items. “Open” and “Closed” are links, not buttons or tabs. A unique searchbox named “Search issues” filters as the user types, including when the entire seeded title is entered. Each result title is a link whose exact accessible name is the title. Open plus the known open title shows that issue and continues to show it after reload; Closed plus the known closed title shows the closed issue and excludes the open issue. A signed-in user with Write, Maintain, or Admin clicks “New issue” from the current repository's Issues list page to open the creation form, enters a non-empty title and optional description, and submits. The system assigns an incrementing unique issue number within the repository, stores the repository identifier, title, description, author, creation time, and Open status, and appends a creation activity; on failure, it does not allocate a number or persist partial data. On success, the system redirects to the new issue detail page, and the list can locate the issue by its new number and status. “New issue” is a link on the Issues page. The creation form has fields labeled “Title” and “Description” and a “Submit new issue” button. A successful submission displays a heading named exactly after the entered title and the exact saved description; reopening the Issues page entry shows a title link with the same exact name. A title containing only three spaces is treated as blank; clicking Submit new issue displays “Title is required” and creates no issue, even if the optional description is empty.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-new-issue.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-5-2-1: Create a Repository Issue - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': \"The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Read and Triage may only view, these edit controls are unavailable, and the server rejects their submissions. The system updates only the title or description of the target issue and stores the editor, edit time, and new value, displaying the change in details, list summaries, and the activity timeline; for an empty title, overlong input, or save failure, the original value is retained. Saving title and description are separate actions; after both saves and reload, the title heading has exactly the new title as its accessible name and the exact new description is visible. The invalid-edit seed is a separate issue with the original title `Original issue title`; replacing its title with three spaces and activating “Save issue title” shows “Title is required”, and reloading restores the original title heading. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-1 (Browse Repository Files and Directories); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Visitors can search from the Code page of a public repository, while signed-in accounts can search any repository they are authorized to view; the results page only reads file content, matching snippets, and paths from currently visible branches of the current repository and cannot leak results across repositories or write code. The repository page and search results expose one searchbox named “Search”; after Enter, a unique link named “Code” selects code results. This results-type link must remain distinguishable from repository navigation so the active search page has only one Code link. Path and language filters are optional, and an unfiltered search can immediately open a result link whose exact accessible name is the matching file name. Seed data provides the known query `search flow`, a matching file `README.md` with that query visible as a complete text value, and the absent query `no-such-token`, which does not occur in searchable code. Opening the matching file shows that text; refreshing preserves the file context, text, and a link named exactly after the file. For the absent query, show “No code results” and retain the exact query in Search. Returning to the repository and repeating the same search produces the same empty state without stale matches.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-repository-code-search.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-4-2-3: Search Code Within a Repository - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.'}, {'keyword': 'THEN', 'content': 'Selecting `main` again restores its file content; switching branches does not create a commit or change either branch head. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.'}]\n\n### Scenario: REQ-4-3-1: List and Switch Repository Branches - Scenario 2\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.'}]\n\n## REQ-4-3-2: Create a Branch from an Existing Revision\n\nThe user opens the branch selector, enters a new branch name, confirms the displayed base branch or commit, and clicks “Create branch: <name>”. As the user types, a valid unused name displays the “Create branch: <name>” option without requiring Enter or a separate search action, while an invalid name immediately displays “Invalid branch”. The system creates a new branch pointing to that base commit and switches the selector to it; if the name already exists or the user lacks permission, no branch is created. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only signed-in users with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status may create branches on repository code and version-control pages; Read and Triage may only browse. The system validates the role, stores the new branch name, base commit, creator, and time, and rejects unauthorized requests. The selector and “Find branch” textbox use REQ-4-3-1's roles. For a valid unused name, the creation entry has role option and accessible name “Create branch: <name>”. Its displayed base defaults to the current branch head; selecting this option performs creation and switches branches without a second mandatory confirmation. Reloading the resulting page entry keeps the newly created branch selected. In particular, entering `invalid..branch` immediately shows “Invalid branch” and cannot create a reference; generated valid names such as `pw-branch-<unique suffix>` follow the same rules.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-create-branch.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-4-3-2: Create a Branch from an Existing Revision - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': \"The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.'}]\n\n## REQ-4-3-3: Change the Repository Default Branch\n\nA repository administrator enters “Settings” → “Branches”, selects an existing branch from the “Default branch” dropdown, clicks “Update”, and then confirms the change. The system saves the new default branch and updates the initial branch shown when the repository is newly opened; it does not delete or rewrite the previous default branch. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only a repository Admin or organization Owner may change the default branch on repository code and version-control pages; other roles may only view. The system validates the role, stores the new default branch together with the operator and time, and rejects unauthorized requests. “Settings” and “Branches” are unique links on their respective pages; “Default branch” is a native HTML select element exposing the combobox role, with options labeled by exact existing branch names; selecting an option must use the native selection behavior. The administrator selects a different seeded branch, activates the “Update” button, and then the “Confirm” button in the confirmation dialog. Opening the repository page entry without a branch then shows “Branch <new default branch name>”; opening that selector still offers an option named exactly after the old default branch. A non-Admin may have Settings or Branches links hidden or may open a read-only settings page, but no “Default branch” combobox or default-branch update button is rendered for that user; a merely disabled editable selector is insufficient.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-default-branch-settings.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-4-3-3: Change the Repository Default Branch - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.'}]\n\n## REQ-4-4: Manage Repository Files Through the Web Interface\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-1 (Browse Repository Files and Directories) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. The file editor is the form opened from Edit or Add file on the Code page; the user edits file content at a path on the current branch and submits it. Only Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner may submit; Read and Triage may only view. A single submission stores the file-path and content change, commit message, author, parent commit, and target branch as one indivisible record and moves the target branch to point to that new commit. A new or renamed file path must not be empty, must not begin with `/`, must not contain a `..` path segment, and must not conflict with an existing file or directory on the current branch; the commit message must contain 1–72 non-empty characters after trimming leading and trailing whitespace. If the path conflicts, content validation fails, the commit message is noncompliant, the branch is protected, or persistence fails, the page displays the reason and does not change the file, branch head, or commit history. An invalid path displays “Invalid file path”, and an empty commit message displays “Commit message is required”. From the writable Code page, the unique “Add file” button opens the “Create new file” menuitem. The editor has a field labeled “File name”, a textbox named “File contents”, an initially empty field labeled “Commit message”, and a “Commit changes” button. Creating a unique file such as `pw-file-<unique suffix>.md` with nonempty text and a message such as `Add <file name>` immediately opens a view displaying the exact saved content. Its “Commits” link opens history displaying the exact submitted message. Submitting `../invalid.md` with content `must not be saved` and no commit message reports the invalid path and/or required message and changes neither files nor history. The example content is ordinary text, not a forbidden content value by itself; rejection follows from the invalid path or missing message.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-web-file-editor.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-4-4: Manage Repository Files Through the Web Interface - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, file `README.md`, commit `Document search flow`.'}]\n\n## REQ-5-1-1: List and Filter Repository Issues\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Users with repository-view permission can read issue rows for the current repository on the Issues list page; each row references a persisted issue number and displays the title, status, author, labels, and update time. Users can filter by Open/Closed, title or body keywords, and labels; when the user types in the issue search box, the matching issue rows update without requiring Enter or a separate search button. Filtering does not create or modify work items. “Open” and “Closed” are links, not buttons or tabs. A unique searchbox named “Search issues” filters as the user types, including when the entire seeded title is entered. Each result title is a link whose exact accessible name is the title. Open plus the known open title shows that issue and continues to show it after reload; Closed plus the known closed title shows the closed issue and excludes the open issue. State and keyword filters combine, and refreshing retains the chosen filter context and matching results.\n\nThe seeded closed issue title is `Legacy welcome text`.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-issues-list.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-5-1-1: List and Filter Repository Issues - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Any user with repository-view permission may read this saved data, but the page displays editable controls only to users with the corresponding role. The seeded open issue title is `Improve onboarding` and its description is `Describe the onboarding improvement.` This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-1 (List and Filter Repository Issues); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-5-1-2: View an Issue and Its Discussion - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. A signed-in user with Write, Maintain, or Admin clicks “New issue” from the current repository's Issues list page to open the creation form, enters a non-empty title and optional description, and submits. The system assigns an incrementing unique issue number within the repository, stores the repository identifier, title, description, author, creation time, and Open status, and appends a creation activity; on failure, it does not allocate a number or persist partial data. On success, the system redirects to the new issue detail page, and the list can locate the issue by its new number and status. “New issue” is a link on the Issues page. The creation form has fields labeled “Title” and “Description” and a “Submit new issue” button. A successful submission displays a heading named exactly after the entered title and the exact saved description; reopening the Issues page entry shows a title link with the same exact name. A title containing only three spaces is treated as blank; clicking Submit new issue displays “Title is required” and creates no issue, even if the optional description is empty.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-new-issue.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-5-2-1: Create a Repository Issue - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': \"The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. A “branch protection rule” is a persistent merge restriction bound to one exact branch name, displayed in the frontend under Settings → Branches, with the backend storing the branch name and rule toggles. This product supports only two independently selectable requirements: “at least 1 valid Approve from someone other than the PR author” and “required check `test` is success”. For a given reviewer on the same current compare commit, only the reviewer's latest decision among Comment, Approve, and Request changes counts. The system blocks direct writes to a protected branch and applies the rule during subsequent PR merging. The repository provides a Settings link followed by a Branches link. For a non-Admin, Add branch protection rule is absent, even if Settings or Branches remain accessible; rejecting a save alone is insufficient. On the PR detail page, the Checks area is available on arrival, displays test: pending initially, and offers the Admin a combobox named test status with a clickable option named success and a Save button. Any user with repository-view permission may filter by Draft/Open/Closed/Merged status, author, or review status; filtering only affects the current list display and does not modify PRs, branches, or reviews. The public list is directly accessible to a visitor and has an Open link for the status filter; selecting it displays the seeded Open PR as a link whose exact accessible name is its title. Opening that link displays the same title as a heading. After reloading the filtered list, that Open PR remains visible; leaving the page and reopening the list, then selecting Open again, shows the same PR. Seed data includes this public list and a stable, uniquely titled Open PR as well as the Closed PR and author used in the filtering scenario. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-pull-requests-list.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-6-2-1: List and Filter Repository Pull Requests - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.'}]\n\n## REQ-6-2-2: Compare Branches Before Opening a Pull Request\n\nThe PR comparison page is a read-only page shown before creation: base is the target branch that receives the merge result, and compare is the source branch that provides the changes to merge. A user with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status clicks “New pull request” on the “Pull requests” page and selects the two branches; the system calculates comparable commits, changed files, and a diff summary based on the commits they currently point to. If the branches are the same or there is no difference, the creation entry is disabled; comparison does not save a PR, commit, or branch change. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only signed-in users with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status may compare branches on the repository pull-request page; Read and Triage may view existing PRs but cannot enter the creation-comparison flow. The system validates the role and rejects unauthorized requests. New pull request is a link. The comparison page has native select controls with combobox roles named base and compare; each option's visible label is its exact branch name. Compare changes is a button. For a valid selection it displays the known changed file path verbatim, a Commit summary with the comparable commit count, and an enabled Create pull request button. Selecting the same branch in both fields immediately displays No changes and disables the creation button, without requiring Compare changes to be clicked first. Clicking Compare changes retains that result. Seed data supplies a contributor with Write permission, a public repository PR-list page entry, a base branch, a distinct compare branch ahead by at least one commit, and a known changed file. Separate valid comparison page entrys open the same usable creation flow with the branches already selected; these pairs have no existing Open or Draft PR at the start of each creation scenario.\n\nThe seeded changed-file path for comparison is `src/search.ts`.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-compare-branches.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-6-2-2: Compare Branches Before Opening a Pull Request - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Its Create pull request button opens a form with a field labeled Title, an optional Description field, and a single Create pull request submit button; the comparison-page action is no longer a competing active button. Successful submission shows the entered title as the exact PR heading and visible Open status, and the title survives reload. A title containing only spaces is rejected with Title is required and creates no PR. A user with Write, Maintain, or Admin clicks “Create pull request” on a valid comparison-results page, enters a title containing 1–256 non-empty characters after trimming leading and trailing whitespace and an optional description of at most 65536 characters, confirms distinct base/compare branches with differences, and clicks “Create pull request”. The system atomically creates an Open PR, stores a unique repository-scoped number, source/target branches, current compare commit, current base commit, title, description, author, creation time, and activity record, and redirects to the detail page; if there is no difference, the branches are the same, a Draft or Open PR with the same source/target pair already exists, the title is empty or overlong, the user lacks permission, or submission fails, no record is created. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-2-2 (Compare Branches Before Opening a Pull Request); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-6-2-3: Create a Pull Request from Comparison Results - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Successful creation visibly shows Draft and a present but disabled Merge pull request button. A dedicated ready-for-review seed PR is separate from draft creation, belongs to the supplied author, initially has no submitted reviews, and displays its title, source branch, and target branch verbatim. Its Ready for review button changes the same PR to Open; if confirmation is used, it is a single Confirm button. Afterward the Draft status marker disappears, the title and branches are unchanged, and a Ready for review activity appears; Open persists on reload. A user with Write, Maintain, or Admin completes a valid base/compare branch selection, clicks “Create draft pull request”, enters a valid title and optional description, and confirms creation. The system creates a PR in Draft state using the same persisted fields as a normal PR and displays a “Draft” marker; a draft cannot submit reviews or be merged. Only the author, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner may click “Ready for review” on a Draft detail page to convert it to Open and record an activity; this transition does not change branches, commits, title, or number. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-2-2 (Compare Branches Before Opening a Pull Request); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-6-2-4: Create a Draft Pull Request - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. A seeded public Open PR is directly viewable without sign-in and displays its exact title in a heading. Commits and Files changed are links; the Commits view displays a Commit summary, and the Files changed view displays a Changed files summary. Reloading preserves the heading and usable navigation links; leaving and reopening the same visible page restores the same PR and allows the same navigation. Seed data includes its stable title, description, discussion comment, and at least one comparable commit. Conversation is the timeline for the author's description, ordinary comments, review summaries, and status events; Commits is the list of commits on the PR's current compare branch relative to base; Files changed is the entry point to the current diff. Users with repository-view permission can switch among the three tabs; all read the same persisted PR, branch, and commit data and do not create comments, reviews, or merges. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-6-2-3 (Create a Pull Request from Comparison Results); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-6-3-1: View Pull Request Overview and Commits - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n\n[Showing lines 595-778 of 877 (50.0KB limit). The system stores the comment identifier, issue identifier, author, body, and creation time, or stores a “subject-target-reaction type” association; for the same user, target, and reaction, only one association is stored, and selecting it a second time removes it. On success, the new comment or reaction is displayed in the discussion and activity timeline; on failure, no local fake comment or partial reaction is displayed. The comment editor is labeled “Comment”, and its submit button is named exactly “Comment”. After submission the complete comment text and author username are visible, and the saved comment remains after reload. Discussion and activity entries use article semantics so an invalid submission cannot add an article; existing entries remain stable across reload. For whitespace-only text the Comment button may either be disabled or remain enabled and display “Comment is required” when activated. Neither path adds a comment or activity. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-5-2-3: Comment on an Issue Discussion - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Matching assignee options update as the user types in the search textbox; pressing Enter or activating a separate search button is not required before selecting an option. Assignable members are accounts that have at least Triage permission on the current repository; the system stores the issue-account relationship, operator, and time and records an activity, while non-assignable users do not appear in results. The settings icon is a button named “Assignees”; the open selector contains a textbox named “Search assignees” and items with role option named exactly after the member username. Seed data provides an eligible member not initially assigned to the target issue. Clicking the member option immediately saves the assignment and closes the selector without a separate Save action; the exact username is visible in metadata and remains after reload. Reopening Assignees shows the selected member without requiring another search; clicking that option again immediately removes the assignment and closes the selector so the username is no longer displayed as an assignee. Historical assignment and unassignment activities remain historical records. Unassignment only deletes that issue relationship and does not delete the member account or repository grant; controls are unavailable to users without the role and the server rejects their submissions. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-5-3-1: Assign or Unassign Issue Participants - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The sidebar has a button named “Labels”, opening options whose exact accessible names are the current repository's label names. Seed data provides an existing label not yet applied to the target issue. Clicking its option immediately saves the association and closes the picker, without requiring a separate Save button; the label name is visible on the detail page and remains after reload. Reopening and selecting the same option removes the association. The label selector must not create labels, display labels from other repositories, or associate labels from other repositories; Read and Write users may only view, the control is unavailable, and the server rejects their submissions. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-5-3-2: Apply Labels to an Issue - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The system stores the association between the work item and milestone and updates detail metadata; it does not create a cross-repository milestone. The settings icon is a button named “Milestone”, and selectable items have role option with the exact milestone name as their accessible name. Seed data provides a selectable milestone belonging to the current repository and an issue not yet associated with it. Clicking the option immediately saves the association and closes the picker with no separate confirmation or save action; the exact milestone name remains visible after reload. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only users with Triage, Maintain, or Admin may use this capability on repository planning and issue-management pages; Read and Write users may only view the current milestone. The system validates the role and rejects unauthorized submissions.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-milestones.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-5-3-3: Assign Issues and Pull Requests to a Milestone - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. The system stores the Open or Closed status, operator, and time, records the transition in activity history, and shows it to subsequent viewers; the status transition must not modify the title, description, comments, labels, assignees, or milestone. The closable seed issue starts Open. The detail page offers a “Close issue” button when Open and a “Reopen issue” button when Closed; activating either immediately saves the change without an additional confirmation. Closing displays Closed status and a “Closed issue” activity, and reopening displays Open status. After reopening and reload the “Close issue” button is available again. On the separate protected issue, the Read viewer sees neither close nor reopen button; hiding both controls is required, in addition to rejecting unauthorized requests. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-5-1-2 (View an Issue and Its Discussion); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\n### Scenario: REQ-5-4: Close or Reopen an Issue - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: repository `acme-docs`, issue `Improve onboarding`, labels `bug` and `documentation`, milestone `Q3 launch`.'}]\n\n## REQ-6-1: Protect Branches with Review and Status-Check Requirements\n\nThis feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. A “branch protection rule” is a persistent merge restriction bound to one exact branch name, displayed in the frontend under Settings → Branches, with the backend storing the branch name and rule toggles. This product supports only two independently selectable requirements: “at least 1 valid Approve from someone other than the PR author” and “required check `test` is success”. For a given reviewer on the same current compare commit, only the reviewer's latest decision among Comment, Approve, and Request changes counts. The PR author cannot satisfy the approval requirement; any valid Request changes blocks merging; when the compare branch gains a new commit, all decisions become stale and a new review is required. “Checks” is the results area on the PR detail page attached to the current compare commit; the `test` status of pending, success, or failure, together with the setter and time, must be stored, and only a repository Admin may update that status from this area. The system blocks direct writes to a protected branch and applies the rule during subsequent PR merging. The repository provides a Settings link followed by a Branches link. The rule form opens from the Add branch protection rule button and contains a field labeled Branch name pattern (an exact branch name, with no wildcard semantics), checkboxes named Require 1 approval and Require status check test, and a Create button; an existing rule uses Save changes. After saving and reloading, the branch name is visible verbatim with the summaries 1 approval and Require status check test. For a non-Admin, Add branch protection rule is absent, even if Settings or Branches remain accessible; rejecting a save alone is insufficient. On the PR detail page, the Checks area is available on arrival, displays test: pending initially, and offers the Admin a combobox named test status with a clickable option named success and a Save button. Saving displays test: success and identifies the setter and time, and reloading preserves the result for that compare commit. Seed data comprises a repository Admin and a readable non-Admin account, a dedicated repository and exact branch name with no existing rule before creation, and a separate Open PR targeting protected main with its current test check initially pending.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-branch-protection.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-6-1: Protect Branches with Review and Status-Check Requirements - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Any user with repository-view permission may filter by Draft/Open/Closed/Merged status, author, or review status; filtering only affects the current list display and does not modify PRs, branches, or reviews. The public list is directly accessible to a visitor and has an Open link for the status filter; selecting it displays the seeded Open PR as a link whose exact accessible name is its title. Opening that link displays the same title as a heading. After reloading the filtered list, that Open PR remains visible; leaving the page and reopening the list, then selecting Open again, shows the same PR. Seed data includes this public list and a stable, uniquely titled Open PR as well as the Closed PR and author used in the filtering scenario. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-3-3 (View a Public Repository Overview); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-pull-requests-list.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-6-2-1: List and Filter Repository Pull Requests - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session. Seed values: pull requests `Improve onboarding` and `Fix search`, branches `main` and `feature-search`, reviewer `bob-reviewer`, check `test`.'}]\n\n## REQ-6-2-2: Compare Branches Before Opening a Pull Request\n\nThe PR comparison page is a read-only page shown before creation: base is the target branch that receives the merge result, and compare is the source branch that provides the changes to merge. A user with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status clicks “New pull request” on the “Pull requests” page and selects the two branches; the system calculates comparable commits, changed files, and a diff summary based on the commits they currently point to. If the branches are the same or there is no difference, the creation entry is disabled; comparison does not save a PR, commit, or branch change. This feature builds on the persisted objects, opened pages, or authenticated-session context provided by REQ-4-3-1 (List and Switch Repository Branches) and REQ-1-1-2 (Sign In with an Existing Account); it must reuse that context and keep pages and data consistent after the context changes. Only signed-in users with Write, Maintain, Admin, or organization Owner status may compare branches on the repository pull-request page; Read and Triage may view existing PRs but cannot enter the creation-comparison flow. The system validates the role and rejects unauthorized requests. New pull request is a link. The comparison page has native select controls with combobox roles named base and compare; each option's visible label is its exact branch name. Compare changes is a button. For a valid selection it displays the known changed file path verbatim, a Commit summary with the comparable commit count, and an enabled Create pull request button. Selecting the same branch in both fields immediately displays No changes and disables the creation button, without requiring Compare changes to be clicked first. Clicking Compare changes retains that result. Seed data supplies a contributor with Write permission, a public repository PR-list page entry, a base branch, a distinct compare branch ahead by at least one commit, and a known changed file. Separate valid comparison page entrys open the same usable creation flow with the branches already selected; these pairs have no existing Open or Draft PR at the start of each creation scenario.\n\nThe seeded changed-file path for comparison is `src/search.ts`.\n\nScreenshot reference:\n![image](reference/github-compare-branches.png)\n\n### Scenario: REQ-6-2-2: Compare Branches Before Opening a Pull Request - Scenario 1\n\n\n\n- steps: [{'keyword': 'GIVEN', 'content': 'The visitor starts at the application home page in a fresh unauthenticated browser session.
